# Sprint Boreas-B — Deployment Automation Spec (scoped to the 2 remaining gaps)

**Status**: DRAFT for codex review (2026-05-23). OP-1133 META.

## Why this spec is small
Boreas-B's original 6-item scope (OP-1133) is **~4/6 already delivered** by the parallel
release-train effort (ADR-0040) + the SLO monitor, not as Boreas-B children. This spec
therefore covers ONLY the 2 genuine remaining Layer-1 gaps. Delivered items, for the record:

| Boreas-B scope item | Delivered by | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 2. Image build→publish→consume + provenance/SBOM | release-train candidate pipeline | cosign sign + SBOM + attest → GitLab CR; v0.5.0 cut pipeline #102 |
| 3. Promotion dev→staging→prod | release-train | candidate→staging→gate→promote-by-digest→prod (v0.5.0 cut); **progressive prod canary still HOLD'd** (OP-931/932/933) — depends on gap #5 below |
| 4. Auto-rollback on health degradation | SLO monitor (OP-883) | F1–F4 + OP-1640/1641 (migration-safe), activated 2026-05-23 |
| 6. Operator override / freeze + manual cut | partial | release:force-promote (ADR-0019/OP-966) + manual cut window + deploy-overlay lock |

**Remaining gaps = items #1 (environment isolation) and #5 (multi-tenant routing).** Both
need operator architecture decisions (the META flagged this as operator-window-top).

---

## Gap A — Environment isolation (Boreas-B scope #1, host-reboot "issue #1")

### Current state
- **dev + prod overlap on the same host** ([[project_prod_environment]]) — the standing risk
  behind the 2026-05-14 host-reboot retro (image/DB drift):
  `docs/retrospectives/2026-05-14-host-reboot-image-db-drift.md`.
- **staging IS isolated** on the same host: separate compose project `omnisight-staging`,
  separate DB `omnisight_staging`, separate ports (55432/18080), isolated volumes
  (`deploy/staging/docker-compose.yml`). This is a proven same-host isolation pattern.
- The danger zone is **dev**: operator edits + runner worktrees + the prod stack share the host
  + (historically) data paths; a careless restart / volume / migration can cross into prod.

### Decisions for the operator (codex to pressure-test)
1. **Isolation boundary**: (a) same-host isolated dev stack (reuse the staging pattern: own
   compose project + DB + ports + volumes), or (b) a separate dev HOST (the local-LLM box?), or
   (c) per-developer ephemeral dev envs. Tradeoff: (a) is cheap + proven (staging already does
   it) but shares kernel/docker/disk with prod; (b) is the real fix but needs another machine.
2. **What "dev" even is here**: the operator's main workdir + the 4 runners currently operate
   against… what? Define the dev data plane (its own PG? a seeded snapshot? read-only against
   prod?) so dev work cannot mutate prod rows.
3. **Runner/coordinator targeting**: how do runners + the pipeline coordinator know they're in
   dev vs prod (env contract, like staging's `verify-env-contract.sh`)? Fail-closed if a dev
   process points at a prod DB URL (staging already rejects prod URLs/live keys).
4. **Boot order / drift guard**: the reboot retro's lesson — env contract + drift gate at boot
   so a wrong-env or image/DB-ahead state fails closed (the /readyz drift gate already does this
   per-stack).

### Proposed ticket family (gap A) — est. 4–6
- A1: dev-env contract + isolated compose project (own PG/ports/volumes), mirroring staging.
- A2: env-contract guard for runners + coordinator (refuse prod DB URL from a dev process).
- A3: dev data seeding (anonymized prod snapshot — note: the OP-972 staging-pg-snapshot tool is
  currently broken/NOTINSTALLED; fix or replace as part of this).
- A4: boot-order + drift-gate verification for the dev stack.
- A5 (if separate-host chosen): provision + cut-over plan.

---

## Gap B — Multi-tenant routing (Boreas-B scope #5; prerequisite for safe canary)

### Current state
- **Tenancy DATA model already exists**: `tenants` table, `tenant_id` columns across many
  tables (git_accounts, sessions, llm_credentials, …), `backend/routers/admin_tenants.py`,
  envelope `tid` in KS.1. So the app is tenant-AWARE at the data layer.
- **Missing = tenant→VERSION routing**: every tenant currently hits the single deployed image
  pair behind Caddy round-robin (backend-a/backend-b, same version). There is no mechanism to
  land a *subset* of tenants on a *new* version — which is exactly what progressive prod canary
  (HOLD'd OP-931/932/933) needs.

### Decisions for the operator (codex to pressure-test)
1. **Routing layer**: where does tenant→version selection happen? (a) Caddy/ingress routes by a
   tenant key (header/cookie/subdomain) to a version-pinned backend pool; (b) a single backend
   pool that self-selects behavior by tenant cohort (feature-flag style, no separate image); (c)
   per-tenant image pinning via the deploy-overlay. Tradeoff: (a) gives true image-level canary
   (matches the release-train digest model) but needs N backend pools; (b) avoids extra pools
   but isn't a real image canary.
2. **Cohort model**: how is a canary cohort defined + advanced? (% of tenants, an explicit
   allowlist, internal-tenants-first?) How does it tie into the release-train promote (a
   `vX.Y.Z` digest serving the canary cohort while prod stays on the previous final)?
3. **Tenant identity at the edge**: how does Caddy/the ingress know the tenant before the
   backend? (subdomain per tenant? a signed cookie? an API key→tenant map?) — this constrains
   option 1a.
4. **Rollback authority**: when the SLO monitor (OP-883) sees a canary-cohort breach, does it
   roll back just the cohort (the existing canary-vs-full decision in slo_monitor) or the whole
   env? The monitor already has a canary branch (currently unreachable because canary is HOLD'd).
5. **Single-host reality**: with dev/prod on one host (Gap A), is a real multi-pool canary even
   worth it yet, or does Gap A come first? (Likely: Gap A is the prerequisite.)

### Proposed ticket family (gap B) — est. 6–10
- B1: tenant identity resolution at the edge (Caddy/ingress) — design + impl.
- B2: version-pinned backend pools (or the chosen routing mechanism from decision 1).
- B3: canary cohort selection + advancement, wired to the release-train promote.
- B4: un-HOLD + repair the progressive-canary orchestrator (OP-931/932/933) on the new routing.
- B5: SLO monitor canary-scoped rollback (the existing canary branch becomes reachable).
- B6: per-tenant version observability (which tenant on which digest — extends /api/version).

---

## Sequencing recommendation
**Gap A (env isolation) BEFORE Gap B (multi-tenant routing).** Real canary on a host where dev
can clobber prod is premature; and the reboot retro makes env isolation the higher-urgency
floor. Gap B is larger + more architectural (a tenancy/routing model that touches the whole
product) and arguably edges into Layer-2 territory — confirm it's still Layer-1-Boreas-B scope
or split it out.

## Open questions for codex
1. Is the 4/6-delivered mapping correct (anything I credited to the release-train that isn't
   actually there)?
2. Gap A: same-host-isolated vs separate-host — which is the minimal-correct floor given the
   reboot retro, and what's the smallest dev-env-contract that prevents dev→prod mutation?
3. Gap B: is true image-level multi-tenant canary (1a, N pools) over-engineering for a
   single-host, ~1-tenant deployment today? Should B be deferred/de-scoped to a feature-flag
   cohort (1b) until there are real multi-tenant + multi-host needs?
4. Sequencing + whether Gap B belongs in Boreas-B (Layer 1) at all vs a later phase.

---

# Boreas-B A2 — dev env-contract guard (design for codex review)

**Ticket**: OP-1643. **Status**: DRAFT for codex adversarial review (2026-05-24).
**Spec parent**: `docs/sprint-boreas/sprint-boreas-b-deployment-automation-spec.md` Gap A2.
**Builds on**: A1/OP-1642 (DB privilege isolation + isolated dev compose stack) — DONE.

## Problem
A1 made dev↔prod isolation *provable at the DB-credential layer* (the `omnisight_dev` role
is `REVOKE`'d `CONNECT` on the prod `omnisight` DB → fail-closed). But that only catches a
process using the `omnisight_dev` credential. The remaining hole is a **dev process that
resolves a prod-looking DATABASE_URL** — e.g. an operator runs a migration / runner / script
from the dev workdir but with the prod `audit-db.env` still sourced, or with `OMNISIGHT_ENV=dev`
set but a stale prod URL. A2 = an env-contract guard that **fails closed** in that case, modeled
on `infra/staging/verify-env-contract.sh`, covering backend + the 4 runners + pipeline-coordinator
+ any alembic/migration entrypoint.

## Key constraint discovered (the design pivot)
Dev and prod backends BOTH connect to their own compose-internal `postgres:5432`. So
**host:port cannot distinguish dev from prod.** The only reliable discriminators are:
- **DB name**: prod = exactly `omnisight`; dev = `omnisight_dev` (ends `_dev`); staging = `*_staging`.
- **DB user**: prod = `omnisight`; dev = `omnisight_dev`.
- **host-published port** (only when a URL targets the host, not compose-internal): dev = 58432.

This mirrors how the staging guard already keys on the `_staging` db-name suffix / staging ports,
not on host. So the guard keys on **db-name + user**, with host-port as a secondary signal.

## Env markers (confirmed from code)
- `OMNISIGHT_ENV`: `dev` (dev compose), `staging` (staging compose), `prod`/`production` (prod;
  `config._resolve_yaml_path` aliases `production→prod`, `development→dev`). **Prod runners +
  coordinator currently run with OMNISIGHT_ENV UNSET** (pipeline-coordinator.service sets none;
  runner wrappers source prod `audit-db.env`, no env marker).

## The contract (truth table)
Let `env = OMNISIGHT_ENV` (canonicalized: production→prod, development/develop→dev), and classify
the resolved DSN as `dev` / `staging` / `prod` / `unknown` by db-name+user+port.

| OMNISIGHT_ENV | DSN shape | Verdict | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dev` | dev | OK | matched |
| `dev` | prod | **FAIL** | the core protection — dev process at prod DB |
| `dev` | staging | **FAIL** | dev process at staging DB |
| `prod` (or production) | prod | OK | matched |
| `prod` | dev / staging | **FAIL** | symmetric — prod process at a non-prod DB |
| *unset* | prod | OK | the current prod runners/coordinator (Exercised AC: prod unaffected) |
| *unset* | dev / staging | **FAIL** | a process at the dev/staging DB that didn't declare itself = misconfig |
| any | sqlite / unknown | SKIP (warn) | legacy SQLite + tests; see exemptions |

**Fail-closed direction**: every "process touching a DB that doesn't match its declared env"
aborts. The only OK-with-unset case is the legacy prod-by-default path (unset + prod DSN), so we
do not break the 4 runners + coordinator as they exist today.

## Two enforcement layers (defense in depth)
1. **In-process guard** — new `backend/config.py::enforce_env_db_contract()` (pure, raises
   `EnvContractViolation`). Called explicitly by each entrypoint:
   - backend: inside `validate_startup_config()` (already invoked in `main.lifespan`, strict when
     `debug=False`) → prod boot refuses on violation.
   - runners: at the top of `auto-runner-jira.py` main.
   - coordinator: at the top of `backend/agents/pipeline_coordinator.py` main.
   - alembic: in `backend/alembic/env.py::_resolve_db_url()` after resolving the URL, before
     handing it to the engine.
   **Why explicit-call, not import-time `sys.exit()`**: a guard at `Settings()` instantiation
   (config import) runs in *every* test that imports `backend.config` (≈ all of them) and would
   abort the suite. Explicit calls at real entrypoints cover all 4 process types without
   poisoning import.
2. **Shell guard** — `infra/dev/verify-env-contract.sh` (mirror of the staging one), wired as
   `ExecStartPre` on the dev compose systemd unit (the A4 unit). Catches a bad dev `.env` before
   the container starts — belt-and-suspenders with layer 1.

## Exemptions (must not break tests / CI / legacy)
The in-process guard is a **no-op** (returns, optionally warns) when ANY of:
- DSN scheme is `sqlite` (legacy/test DBs) or empty/unknown.
- `PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST` is set (running under pytest) **OR** `settings.ci_mode` is true.
- explicit escape hatch `OMNISIGHT_ENV_CONTRACT_DISABLE=1` (documented break-glass; logged loudly).

## Classification helper (shared)
`_classify_dsn(dsn) -> Literal["dev","staging","prod","sqlite","unknown"]`:
- parse db-name = last path segment minus `?query`; parse user from `//user:...@`.
- `sqlite` if scheme sqlite. `dev` if db-name ends `_dev`/`-dev` OR user == `omnisight_dev` OR
  host-port 58432. `staging` if ends `_staging`/`-staging` OR staging ports 55432/55433. `prod` if
  db-name is exactly `omnisight` AND not dev/staging. else `unknown` (treated like sqlite: skip+warn,
  since we can't prove a violation — fail-closed only on a *positive* mismatch, to avoid breaking
  unforeseen valid DSNs).

## AC mapping (4-AC)
- **Code**: `enforce_env_db_contract()` + `_classify_dsn()` in config.py; `infra/dev/verify-env-contract.sh`.
- **Deploy**: dev compose unit references the shell guard as ExecStartPre (lands with A4 unit);
  alembic/runner/coordinator entrypoints call the in-process guard.
- **Integration**: a process with `OMNISIGHT_ENV=dev` + prod DSN aborts with `EnvContractViolation`
  (unit test over the truth table) + the shell guard rejects a prod-DSN dev `.env`.
- **Exercised**: a prod process (env unset OR prod) + prod DSN starts clean; the live prod backend
  /readyz stays green after the guard ships (no false-positive on the real prod config).

## Open questions for codex
1. Truth-table holes — is "unset + dev DSN → FAIL" safe, or is there a legitimate flow (a dev-DB
   integration test run outside pytest?) it would wrongly kill? Should "unknown" ever be a hard fail?
2. Is keying `prod` as "db-name == exactly `omnisight`" too brittle (what if prod DB gets renamed,
   or a tenant DB `omnisight_<tid>` appears)? Better positive prod signal?
3. The in-process guard runs at *import-adjacent* entrypoints but NOT at bare `python -c "import
   backend.config"`. Is the explicit-call coverage (4 sites) actually complete, or is there a 5th
   entrypoint (e.g. a one-off `scripts/*.py` that opens the DB directly via `backend.db`)? Should
   the chokepoint instead be `backend/db.py::_resolve_pg_dsn()` / pool init (runs whenever ANYONE
   actually opens a connection) rather than per-entrypoint?
4. Escape-hatch + pytest exemption: any risk the exemptions become a silent bypass in prod (e.g.
   PYTEST_CURRENT_TEST leaking into a prod shell)? Should the escape hatch itself be refused when
   env==prod?
5. Should the guard also assert the *converse* for the dev stack at the shell layer (dev `.env`
   must NOT carry prod/live API keys), mirroring staging's Stripe/Anthropic/Slack sandbox-prefix
   checks? Or is that out of A2 scope (A2 is DB-URL-focused)?

#!/usr/bin/env bash
#
# [OP-971] AUDIT-19a — staging env contract verifier.
#
# Runs as ExecStartPre of deploy/systemd/omnisight-staging-compose.service
# (and is safe to run by hand: `infra/staging/verify-env-contract.sh`).
# It is the guard that stops a fat-fingered staging .env / .env.local from
# bringing the staging stack up against PRODUCTION resources on the
# co-tenanted 5a host.
#
# It refuses to start the stack (exit 1, error code EnvContractViolation)
# if ANY of these hold:
#
#   1. The Postgres DSN does not look like staging — its database-name
#      component does not end with `-staging` / `_staging` AND it does not
#      connect on a known staging port (55432 / 55433). i.e. it looks like
#      it is pointed at prod Postgres.
#   2. An external API key is missing its sandbox prefix:
#        - Stripe         STRIPE_SECRET_KEY            must start `sk_test_`
#                         (a `sk_live_` key is an instant fail)
#        - Anthropic      ANTHROPIC_API_KEY /
#                         OMNISIGHT_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY  must be empty or a
#                         test/sandbox/eval key (name contains test|sandbox|eval)
#        - Slack          SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL /
#                         OMNISIGHT_STAGING_ALERT_WEBHOOK
#                         must be empty or a staging webhook (URL contains
#                         "staging")
#   3. `environment=staging` is not exported (the lowercase var the ticket
#      pins; we also accept OMNISIGHT_ENV=staging as the canonical alias).
#
# Two more checks emit a *warning* by default but do not block — the
# systemd unit's `up -d --wait` surfaces a real port collision anyway,
# and the cgroup limits failing to apply is logged by the user manager:
#
#   - PortCollisionWithProd — a configured staging port is already bound.
#     Override the port in deploy/staging/.env / .env.local. Set
#     OMNISIGHT_STAGING_STRICT_PORTS=1 to make this a hard fail (exit 2).
#   - CgroupV1Fallback — the kernel exposes only cgroup v1, so the unit's
#     MemoryMax=30% / CPUQuota=30% percentage limits will not take effect.
#     Set OMNISIGHT_STAGING_REQUIRE_CGROUP_V2=1 to make this a hard fail,
#     or follow the runbook to switch the unit to absolute byte limits.
#
# Inputs (all optional, env-overridable):
#   OMNISIGHT_STAGING_ENV_FILE   default /home/user/sora-bridge/deploy/staging/.env
#                                also reads a sibling `.env.local` if present
#   OMNISIGHT_STAGING_HEALTHZ_PORT          default 18080
#   OMNISIGHT_STAGING_EXTRA_PORTS           space-separated extra ports to check
#   OMNISIGHT_STAGING_STRICT_PORTS          "1" => PortCollisionWithProd is fatal
#   OMNISIGHT_STAGING_REQUIRE_CGROUP_V2     "1" => CgroupV1Fallback is fatal
#
# Exit codes:  0 ok   1 EnvContractViolation
#              2 PortCollisionWithProd (only when STRICT_PORTS=1)
#              3 CgroupV1Fallback      (only when REQUIRE_CGROUP_V2=1)

set -euo pipefail

SELF="verify-env-contract.sh"
ENV_FILE="${OMNISIGHT_STAGING_ENV_FILE:-/home/user/sora-bridge/deploy/staging/.env}"
HEALTHZ_PORT="${OMNISIGHT_STAGING_HEALTHZ_PORT:-18080}"
STAGING_PG_PORTS=(55432 55433)

log()  { printf '[%s] %s\n' "$SELF" "$*" >&2; }
warn() { printf '[%s] WARNING: %s\n' "$SELF" "$*" >&2; }
fail() {
	# $1 = error code (EnvContractViolation|PortCollisionWithProd|CgroupV1Fallback)
	# $2 = human detail ; $3 = exit status
	printf '[%s] %s: %s\n' "$SELF" "$1" "$2" >&2
	exit "${3:-1}"
}

# ── Load env from the staging env file(s) into this shell ─────────────
# We deliberately read the file rather than trusting only the inherited
# environment: docker compose reads `deploy/staging/.env` directly, so the
# contract has to inspect the same source of truth. `.env.local` (if any)
# layers on top, matching compose's --env-file precedence convention.
load_env_file() {
	local f="$1"
	[[ -f "$f" ]] || return 0
	log "loading env from $f"
	# Only accept simple KEY=VALUE lines; ignore comments / blanks / exports.
	while IFS= read -r line || [[ -n "$line" ]]; do
		line="${line#"${line%%[![:space:]]*}"}"          # ltrim
		[[ -z "$line" || "$line" == \#* ]] && continue
		[[ "$line" == export\ * ]] && line="${line#export }"
		[[ "$line" == *=* ]] || continue
		local key="${line%%=*}" val="${line#*=}"
		key="${key%"${key##*[![:space:]]}"}"             # rtrim key
		# strip a single layer of matching surrounding quotes
		if [[ "$val" == \"*\" || "$val" == \'*\' ]]; then val="${val:1:${#val}-2}"; fi
		[[ "$key" =~ ^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$ ]] || continue
		# Inherited environment wins over the file (systemd EnvironmentFile,
		# operator `KEY=v ./verify-env-contract.sh`), so only set if unset.
		if [[ -z "${!key+x}" ]]; then export "$key=$val"; fi
	done < "$f"
}

load_env_file "$ENV_FILE"
load_env_file "${ENV_FILE}.local"
load_env_file "$(dirname "$ENV_FILE")/.env.local"

# ── 1. Postgres DSN must look like staging ────────────────────────────
# Accept the full SQLAlchemy URL (OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL / DATABASE_URL)
# or the decomposed POSTGRES_DB + STAGING_POSTGRES_PORT used by the compose
# file's defaults.
dsn_is_staging() {
	local dsn="$1"
	[[ -n "$dsn" ]] || return 1
	# database name = last path segment, minus any ?query
	local dbpart="${dsn##*/}"; dbpart="${dbpart%%\?*}"
	case "$dbpart" in
		*-staging|*_staging|*-staging-*|staging_*|staging-*) return 0 ;;
	esac
	# or: connects on a known staging Postgres port
	local p
	for p in "${STAGING_PG_PORTS[@]}"; do
		[[ "$dsn" == *":$p/"* || "$dsn" == *":$p?"* || "$dsn" == *":$p" ]] && return 0
	done
	return 1
}

DSN="${OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL:-${DATABASE_URL:-}}"
if [[ -z "$DSN" && -n "${POSTGRES_DB:-}" ]]; then
	# Reconstruct just enough of a DSN to validate the db-name + port.
	DSN="postgresql://x@${POSTGRES_HOST:-postgres}:${STAGING_POSTGRES_PORT:-5432}/${POSTGRES_DB}"
fi
if [[ -z "$DSN" ]]; then
	fail EnvContractViolation \
		"no Postgres DSN found (set OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL or POSTGRES_DB in $ENV_FILE)" 1
fi
if ! dsn_is_staging "$DSN"; then
	# Redact credentials before echoing the DSN back.
	redacted="${DSN/\/\/*@/\/\/***@}"
	fail EnvContractViolation \
		"Postgres DSN does not look like staging — database name must end with -staging/_staging or use a staging port (${STAGING_PG_PORTS[*]}); refusing to start staging against what looks like prod Postgres: $redacted" 1
fi
log "ok: Postgres DSN looks like staging"

# ── 2. External API keys must carry their sandbox prefixes ────────────
key_starts_with() { [[ "$1" == "$2"* ]]; }
contains_ci() {
	# case-insensitive substring
	local hay="${1,,}" needle="${2,,}"
	[[ "$hay" == *"$needle"* ]]
}

# Stripe — sk_test_ required; sk_live_ is an instant fail.
if [[ -n "${STRIPE_SECRET_KEY:-}" ]]; then
	if key_starts_with "$STRIPE_SECRET_KEY" "sk_live_"; then
		fail EnvContractViolation "STRIPE_SECRET_KEY is a LIVE key (sk_live_) — staging must use sk_test_" 1
	fi
	if ! key_starts_with "$STRIPE_SECRET_KEY" "sk_test_"; then
		fail EnvContractViolation "STRIPE_SECRET_KEY missing sandbox prefix — must start with sk_test_" 1
	fi
	log "ok: Stripe key is a test key"
fi

# Anthropic — empty, or a key whose name marks it test/sandbox/eval.
for var in ANTHROPIC_API_KEY OMNISIGHT_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY; do
	val="${!var:-}"
	[[ -n "$val" ]] || continue
	if contains_ci "$val" "test" || contains_ci "$val" "sandbox" || contains_ci "$val" "eval"; then
		log "ok: $var looks like a non-prod Anthropic key"
	else
		fail EnvContractViolation "$var does not look like a test/sandbox Anthropic key — staging must not use a production Anthropic key" 1
	fi
done

# Slack — empty, or a webhook URL that names staging.
for var in SLACK_WEBHOOK_URL OMNISIGHT_STAGING_ALERT_WEBHOOK SLACK_ALERT_WEBHOOK; do
	val="${!var:-}"
	[[ -n "$val" ]] || continue
	if contains_ci "$val" "staging"; then
		log "ok: $var is a staging Slack webhook"
	else
		fail EnvContractViolation "$var is not a staging Slack webhook (URL must contain 'staging')" 1
	fi
done

# ── 3. environment=staging must be exported ───────────────────────────
ENV_MARKER="${environment:-${OMNISIGHT_ENV:-}}"
if [[ "$ENV_MARKER" != "staging" ]]; then
	fail EnvContractViolation \
		"environment=staging is not exported (got environment='${environment:-}', OMNISIGHT_ENV='${OMNISIGHT_ENV:-}')" 1
fi
log "ok: environment=staging is set"

# ── 4. (warn) Port collisions with whatever else runs on the box ──────
port_in_use() {
	local p="$1"
	if command -v ss >/dev/null 2>&1; then
		ss -ltnH "( sport = :$p )" 2>/dev/null | grep -q . && return 0 || return 1
	fi
	# Fallback: bash /dev/tcp probe to localhost.
	( exec 3<>"/dev/tcp/127.0.0.1/$p" ) >/dev/null 2>&1 && { exec 3>&- 3<&- 2>/dev/null || true; return 0; }
	return 1
}
CHECK_PORTS=("$HEALTHZ_PORT" "${STAGING_HTTP_PORT:-18080}" "${STAGING_BACKEND_A_PORT:-8010}" \
	"${STAGING_BACKEND_B_PORT:-8011}" "${STAGING_POSTGRES_PORT:-55432}" ${OMNISIGHT_STAGING_EXTRA_PORTS:-})
collision_ports=()
for p in "${CHECK_PORTS[@]}"; do
	[[ "$p" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] || continue
	if port_in_use "$p"; then collision_ports+=("$p"); fi
done
if [[ "${#collision_ports[@]}" -gt 0 ]]; then
	detail="port(s) already bound: ${collision_ports[*]} — override the matching STAGING_*_PORT in $ENV_FILE / .env.local"
	if [[ "${OMNISIGHT_STAGING_STRICT_PORTS:-0}" == "1" ]]; then
		fail PortCollisionWithProd "$detail" 2
	fi
	warn "PortCollisionWithProd: $detail (will surface again as a bind error in \`up -d --wait\` if real)"
else
	log "ok: no obvious staging port collisions"
fi

# ── 5. (warn / opt-in fatal) cgroup v2 needed for percentage limits ───
if [[ -e /sys/fs/cgroup/cgroup.controllers ]]; then
	log "ok: cgroup v2 unified hierarchy present — MemoryMax=30%/CPUQuota=30% will apply"
else
	if [[ "${OMNISIGHT_STAGING_REQUIRE_CGROUP_V2:-0}" == "1" ]]; then
		fail CgroupV1Fallback \
			"kernel exposes only cgroup v1 — the unit's MemoryMax=30%/CPUQuota=30% would silently no-op; set absolute byte limits per the runbook or unset OMNISIGHT_STAGING_REQUIRE_CGROUP_V2" 3
	fi
	warn "CgroupV1Fallback: cgroup v2 not detected — omnisight-staging-compose.service percentage limits will NOT take effect; see docs/operations/staging-environment-runbook.md (CgroupV1Fallback)"
fi

log "env contract OK — staging may start"
exit 0

   780	# halves are kept disjoint by :data:`backend.secrets_provider.
   781	# SECRET_FIELDS` — the migration script enforces the split, and
   782	# the overlay below refuses to apply a YAML entry that names a
   783	# secret field.
   784	#
   785	# Resolution order applied below (lowest → highest):
   786	#
   787	#   1. ``Settings`` class default
   788	#   2. Vault secret (when ``OMNISIGHT_SECRETS_BACKEND != env``)
   789	#   3. ``config/<env>.yaml`` value for non-secret fields
   790	#   4. ``.env`` value (legacy; pydantic loads after step 5 has run)
   791	#   5. Process env (shell / docker --env / deploy ``--override``)
   792	#
   793	# The overlay function writes into ``os.environ`` BEFORE the
   794	# ``Settings()`` singleton is instantiated, and only when the env
   795	# var is NOT already set — that ordering preserves rule 5 as the
   796	# top-priority path so a deploy ``--override`` still wins over the
   797	# YAML default.
   798	
   799	
   800	def _resolve_yaml_path(env_name: str) -> Path | None:
   801	    """Map ``env`` to ``config/<env>.yaml`` next to the repo root.
   802	
   803	    Accepts ``production`` as an alias for ``prod`` so production
   804	    hosts can keep ``OMNISIGHT_ENV=production`` (the value the
   805	    startup-config validator already special-cases) without us
   806	    having to ship a redundant ``config/production.yaml``.
   807	    """
   808	    aliases = {"production": "prod", "develop": "dev", "development": "dev"}
   809	    canonical = aliases.get(env_name, env_name)
   810	    repo_root = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
   811	    candidate = repo_root / "config" / f"{canonical}.yaml"
   812	    return candidate if candidate.exists() else None
   813	
   814	
   815	def _set_env_if_unset(field_name: str, value: str) -> bool:
   816	    """Set ``OMNISIGHT_<UPPER>`` in ``os.environ`` if absent.
   817	
   818	    Returns True when the env was actually set, so the caller can
   819	    log the overlay decision deterministically. Empty / None
   820	    values are skipped — a yaml key that left the value empty is
   821	    semantically "fall through to next layer".
   822	    """
   823	    if value is None or value == "":
   824	        return False
   825	    env_name = f"OMNISIGHT_{field_name.upper()}"
   826	    if env_name in os.environ:
   827	        return False
   828	    os.environ[env_name] = str(value)
   829	    return True
   830	
   831	
   832	def _apply_yaml_overlay(yaml_path: Path) -> int:
   833	    """Inject non-secret YAML fields into ``os.environ``.
   834	
   835	    Returns the number of env vars actually written (i.e. excludes
   836	    keys that were already set via shell / .env, and excludes
   837	    secret-field entries which are rejected outright).
   838	
   839	    A yaml entry that names a key not declared on ``Settings`` is
   840	    logged at WARNING and skipped — pydantic-settings'
   841	    ``extra='forbid'`` would reject it on instantiation anyway, but
   842	    the explicit log line makes the source of the boot failure
   843	    obvious.
   844	    """
   845	    try:
   846	        import yaml as _yaml  # type: ignore[import-not-found]
   847	    except ImportError:
   848	        _startup_logger.error(
   849	            "PyYAML not installed; cannot apply config overlay from %s. "
   850	            "Install ``pyyaml`` or unset OMNISIGHT_ENV.",
   851	            yaml_path,
   852	        )
   853	        return 0
   854	
   855	    try:
   856	        raw = _yaml.safe_load(yaml_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")) or {}
   857	    except Exception as exc:
   858	        _startup_logger.error("config: failed to parse %s: %s", yaml_path, exc)
   859	        return 0
   860	    if not isinstance(raw, dict):
   861	        _startup_logger.error(
   862	            "config: %s top-level must be a mapping, got %s",
   863	            yaml_path, type(raw).__name__,
   864	        )
   865	        return 0
   866	
   867	    from backend.secrets_provider import is_secret_field
   868	    field_names = set(Settings.model_fields)
   869	    written = 0
   870	    for key, value in raw.items():
   871	        key = str(key)
   872	        if key not in field_names:
   873	            _startup_logger.warning(
   874	                "config: %s declares unknown Settings field %r — skipping",
   875	                yaml_path, key,
   876	            )
   877	            continue
   878	        if is_secret_field(key):
   879	            _startup_logger.error(
   880	                "config: %s declares secret field %r — secrets must be "
   881	                "in the vault, NEVER in checked-in YAML. Skipping.",
   882	                yaml_path, key,
   883	            )
   884	            continue
   885	        if isinstance(value, bool):
   886	            stringified = "true" if value else "false"
   887	        elif value is None:
   888	            continue
   889	        else:
   890	            stringified = str(value)
   891	        if _set_env_if_unset(key, stringified):
   892	            written += 1
   893	    return written
   894	
   895	
   896	def _apply_secrets_overlay() -> int:
   897	    """Pull secrets from the configured provider into ``os.environ``.
   898	
   899	    Returns the count of env vars actually written. The default
   900	    backend ``env`` is a no-op (the value is already where pydantic
   901	    will look). Misconfiguration (bad backend name, unreachable
   902	    Vault, undecryptable file) is logged at ERROR and re-raised so
   903	    the boot fails loudly instead of silently falling back to
   904	    plaintext .env values.
   905	    """
   906	    backend_name = (os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_SECRETS_BACKEND") or "env").strip().lower()
   907	    if backend_name == "env":
   908	        return 0
   909	    from backend.secrets_provider import make_secrets_provider
   910	    provider = make_secrets_provider(backend_name)
   911	    written = 0
   912	    for key in provider.list_keys():
   913	        value = provider.get(key)
   914	        if value and _set_env_if_unset(key, value):
   915	            written += 1
   916	    _startup_logger.info(
   917	        "config: secrets overlay (%s backend) applied %d value(s)",
   918	        backend_name, written,
   919	    )
   920	    return written
   921	
   922	
   923	def _apply_env_overlay() -> None:
   924	    """Apply YAML + vault overlays to ``os.environ`` before Settings().
   925	
   926	    Idempotent — re-runs in tests by toggling
   927	    ``OMNISIGHT_ENV`` / ``OMNISIGHT_SECRETS_BACKEND`` only affect
   928	    keys not already set. Tests that need to flip the overlay should
   929	    monkeypatch the env, ``del`` any prior writes from the registry,
   930	    and re-import ``backend.config``.
   931	    """
   932	    env_name = (os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_ENV") or "").strip().lower()
   933	    if env_name:
   934	        yaml_path = _resolve_yaml_path(env_name)
   935	        if yaml_path is not None:
   936	            applied = _apply_yaml_overlay(yaml_path)
   937	            _startup_logger.info(
   938	                "config: yaml overlay %s applied %d value(s)",
   939	                yaml_path.name, applied,
   940	            )
   941	        else:
   942	            _startup_logger.info(
   943	                "config: OMNISIGHT_ENV=%s but no config/<env>.yaml found; "
   944	                "running on env vars only", env_name,
   945	            )
   946	    _apply_secrets_overlay()
   947

   955	#  Phase 5-10 (#multi-account-forge) legacy credential deprecation

   957	#
   958	# Phase 5-1 through 5-9 shipped the ``git_accounts`` table + CRUD API
   959	# + UI as the authoritative source for per-tenant, multi-account forge
   960	# credentials. The ``Settings`` scalar / JSON-map fields below are the
   961	# previous generation and are now deprecated:
   962	#
   963	#   * READ stays OK — ``backend.git_credentials._build_registry`` still
   964	#     synthesises a virtual ``git_accounts``-shaped row from whichever
   965	#     of these is set, so operator deployments that never populated
   966	#     ``git_accounts`` keep working unchanged until Phase 5-5's
   967	#     lifespan auto-migration converts them on next backend boot.
   968	#   * WRITE (via ``PUT /runtime/settings``) is considered deprecated.
   969	#     Callers that write a legacy field emit an ``audit.log`` row with
   970	#     ``action=settings.legacy_credential_write`` so the trail records
   971	#     *who* kept the old UI alive; the response also carries a
   972	#     ``deprecations`` block so the frontend can surface a yellow
   973	#     "move to SYSTEM INTEGRATIONS → Git Accounts" banner.
   974	#
   975	# The registry is a plain ``dict[str, str]`` mapping the Settings field
   976	# name to a short replacement hint — the hint is what shows up in the
   977	# audit-log ``after`` dict and the PUT response's ``deprecations`` key,
   978	# so it needs to be accurate (grep-able when debugging later) but
   979	# short (UI surface).
   980	#
   981	# Fields intentionally NOT listed here (and why):
   982	#   * ``gerrit_enabled`` — master switch / feature flag, not a
   983	#     credential. Stays as a Settings scalar even after migration.
   984	#   * ``gerrit_replication_targets`` — list of git *destinations*
   985	#     (post-merge push), not a credential. See Phase 5-7 HANDOFF.
   986	#   * ``jira_intake_label`` / ``jira_done_statuses`` — routing knobs
   987	#     for JIRA inbound automation, not the JIRA credential itself.
   988	#   * ``ollama_base_url`` / ``*_api_key`` — LLM-provider credentials.
   989	#     Phase 5b has a separate ``llm_credentials`` table; those
   990	#     deprecation hooks land with 5b-6, not here.

  1020	def is_legacy_credential_field(name: str) -> bool:
  1021	    """Return True when ``name`` is a Phase-5-deprecated credential
  1022	    field on the ``Settings`` singleton.
  1023	
  1024	    Use this at write sites (``PUT /runtime/settings``, wizard /
  1025	    rotate helpers) to decide whether to emit
  1026	    ``audit.log(action="settings.legacy_credential_write", ...)``.
  1027	    Read sites MUST NOT gate on this — reads still resolve through
  1028	    the legacy shim in ``backend.git_credentials``.
  1029	    """
  1030	    return name in LEGACY_CREDENTIAL_FIELDS

  1034	#  Phase 5b-6 (#llm-credentials) LLM credential deprecation
  1035	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
  1036	#
  1037	# Phase 5b-1 through 5b-5 shipped the ``llm_credentials`` table +
  1038	# CRUD API + UI + lifespan auto-migration as the authoritative source
  1039	# for per-tenant, multi-account LLM provider credentials. The
  1040	# ``Settings`` scalar fields below are the previous generation and
  1041	# are now deprecated:
  1042	#
  1043	#   * READ stays OK — ``backend.llm_credential_resolver`` falls back
  1044	#     to ``settings.{provider}_api_key`` when the DB has no row for
  1045	#     a provider, so deployments that never populated
  1046	#     ``llm_credentials`` keep working unchanged. Phase 5b-5's
  1047	#     lifespan auto-migration converts them on next backend boot
  1048	#     unless ``OMNISIGHT_LLM_CREDENTIAL_MIGRATE=skip`` is set.
  1049	#   * WRITE via ``PUT /runtime/settings`` is now REJECTED. The
  1050	#     ``_UPDATABLE_FIELDS`` whitelist in
  1051	#     :mod:`backend.routers.integration` no longer lists these
  1052	#     fields, so writes return ``rejected[field]="deprecated: use
  1053	#     POST /api/v1/llm-credentials"`` + emit an ``audit.log`` row
  1054	#     with ``action=settings.legacy_llm_credential_write`` + the
  1055	#     response carries a ``deprecations`` block the UI can surface.
  1056	#
  1057	# Fields intentionally NOT listed here (and why):
  1058	#   * ``llm_provider`` / ``llm_model`` / ``llm_temperature`` /
  1059	#     ``llm_fallback_chain`` — routing / selection knobs, not
  1060	#     credentials. Still go through the Settings path in
  1061	#     Phase 5b-6; future phases may promote them to DB.
  1062	#   * ``token_*`` budget knobs — observability configuration, not
  1063	#     a credential.
  1064	#   * Git-forge credentials (``github_token`` / ``gerrit_url`` /
  1065	#     ...) — those are Phase 5-10 scope and live in
  1066	#     ``LEGACY_CREDENTIAL_FIELDS`` above.
  1067	
  1068	LEGACY_LLM_CREDENTIAL_FIELDS: dict[str, str] = {
  1069	    "anthropic_api_key":  "llm_credentials(provider='anthropic').encrypted_value",
  1070	    "google_api_key":     "llm_credentials(provider='google').encrypted_value",
  1071	    "openai_api_key":     "llm_credentials(provider='openai').encrypted_value",
  1072	    "xai_api_key":        "llm_credentials(provider='xai').encrypted_value",
  1073	    "groq_api_key":       "llm_credentials(provider='groq').encrypted_value",
  1074	    "deepseek_api_key":   "llm_credentials(provider='deepseek').encrypted_value",
  1075	    "together_api_key":   "llm_credentials(provider='together').encrypted_value",
  1076	    "openrouter_api_key": "llm_credentials(provider='openrouter').encrypted_value",
  1077	    # Not a secret per se but same lifecycle: 5b-4 UI moved it into
  1078	    # ``llm_credentials(provider='ollama').metadata.base_url``.
  1079	    "ollama_base_url":    "llm_credentials(provider='ollama').metadata.base_url",
  1080	}
  1081	
  1082	
  1083	def is_legacy_llm_credential_field(name: str) -> bool:
  1084	    """Return True when ``name`` is a Phase-5b-deprecated LLM
  1085	    credential field on the ``Settings`` singleton.
  1086	
  1087	    Use this at write sites (``PUT /runtime/settings``) to decide
  1088	    whether to reject + emit ``audit.log(action=
  1089	    "settings.legacy_llm_credential_write", ...)``. Read sites MUST
  1090	    NOT gate on this — reads still fall back through
  1091	    :mod:`backend.llm_credential_resolver`.
  1092	    """
  1093	    return name in LEGACY_LLM_CREDENTIAL_FIELDS
  1094	
  1095	
  1096	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
  1097	#  L1-03: startup-time config validation
  1098	# ━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
  1099	#
  1100	# Called from backend.main lifespan. Catches common deploy-day
  1101	# mistakes that would otherwise manifest as confusing 401s or
  1102	# silent "provider unreachable" logs hours later.
  1103	
  1104	def _mask(v: str, show: int = 4) -> str:
  1105	    """Redact secrets for logging: keep first + last `show` chars."""
  1106	    if not v:
  1107	        return "(empty)"
  1108	    if len(v) <= show * 2 + 3:
  1109	        return "***"
  1110	    return f"{v[:show]}…{v[-show:]}"
  1111	
  1112	
  1113	# Provider key prefixes we recognise. Wrong prefix = almost certainly
  1114	# a copy-paste error; we warn but don't block so private deployments
  1115	# (proxies, Bedrock-style wrappers) still work.
  1116	_PROVIDER_PREFIXES: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {
  1117	    "anthropic_api_key":  ("sk-ant-",),
  1118	    "openai_api_key":     ("sk-", "sk-proj-"),
  1119	    "google_api_key":     (),            # Google keys are varied
  1120	    "xai_api_key":        ("xai-",),
  1121	    "groq_api_key":       ("gsk_",),
  1122	    "deepseek_api_key":   ("sk-",),
  1123	    "together_api_key":   (),            # Together keys are varied
  1124	    "openrouter_api_key": ("sk-or-",),
  1125	}
  1126	
  1127	_MIN_BEARER_LEN = 16  # 128-bit entropy, roughly
  1128	
  1129	
  1130	class ConfigValidationError(SystemExit):
  1131	    """Startup-time settings rejected — refuse to boot (exit 78 = EX_CONFIG)."""
  1132	    def __init__(self, message: str):
  1133	        super().__init__(78)
  1134	        self.message = message
  1135	
  1136	    def __str__(self) -> str:
  1137	        return self.message
  1138	
  1139	
  1140	def validate_startup_config(strict: bool | None = None) -> list[str]:
  1141	    """Sanity-check the loaded settings + critical env vars. Returns a
  1142	    list of warnings (empty = clean). Raises `ConfigValidationError`
  1143	    on a *hard* problem if strict mode is on.
  1144	
  1145	    `strict` defaults to True when *neither* `settings.debug` nor
  1146	    `settings.ci_mode` is set, False when either is on — dev workflow
  1147	    stays lenient, CI runners stay lenient (H7 audit: preferred over
  1148	    re-purposing DEBUG for CI), prod boots refuse to start with a
  1149	    known-dangerous config.
  1150	    """
  1151	    if strict is None:
  1152	        strict = not (settings.debug or settings.ci_mode)
  1153	
  1154	    warnings: list[str] = []
  1155	    hard_errors: list[str] = []
  1156	
  1157	    # ── Bearer token ──
  1158	    # FX2.D4.4 (OP-237, 2026-05-16): OMNISIGHT_DECISION_BEARER is on the
  1159	    # sunset path. K6 ``api_keys`` table is the supported replacement —
  1160	    # ``migrate_legacy_bearer()`` at lifespan startup moves the env value
  1161	    # into a hashed ``api_keys`` row (id ``ak-legacy-<sha[:12]>``), and
  1162	    # operators should create per-service keys via Admin UI > API Keys.
  1163	    # Removal is targeted at the next major release; until then the var
  1164	    # is honoured for backwards compat but every prod boot that still
  1165	    # carries it emits a deprecation warning so the signal can't be
  1166	    # silently ignored.
  1167	    bearer = (os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_DECISION_BEARER") or "").strip()
  1168	    if bearer:
  1169	        if len(bearer) < _MIN_BEARER_LEN:
  1170	            msg = (
  1171	                f"OMNISIGHT_DECISION_BEARER is only {len(bearer)} chars "
  1172	                f"(min {_MIN_BEARER_LEN}). Brute-forcing is cheap at "
  1173	                "that size — use at least 16."
  1174	            )
  1175	            (hard_errors if strict else warnings).append(msg)
  1176	        warnings.append(
  1177	            "OMNISIGHT_DECISION_BEARER is DEPRECATED and scheduled for "
  1178	            "removal in the next major release. K6 has auto-migrated it "
  1179	            "into the api_keys table; create per-service keys via "
  1180	            "Admin UI > API Keys, then unset the env var. See "
  1181	            "docs/ops/security_baseline.md §3 + "
  1182	            "docs/security/as_0_4_credential_refactor_migration_plan.md "
  1183	            "Track B for the migration path."
  1184	        )
  1185	    else:
  1186	        # Empty bearer leaves DE mutator endpoints open. Fine in dev,
  1187	        # foot-gun in prod. Audit H1 (2026-04-19): upgrade to hard error
  1188	        # under strict mode — mirrors C1's admin-password treatment, so
  1189	        # a production deploy that forgets the bearer env can't silently
  1190	        # ship open mutator endpoints.
  1191	        #
  1192	        # FX2.D4.4 (OP-237): once the legacy env is gone, operators are
  1193	        # expected to gate mutators via api_keys entries instead; the
  1194	        # "empty bearer = open" alarm stays for the deprecation window
  1195	        # so deployments that simply unset the var without creating
  1196	        # api_keys rows still get caught at startup.
  1197	        msg = (
  1198	            "OMNISIGHT_DECISION_BEARER is empty — Decision Engine "
  1199	            "mutator endpoints (approve/reject/undo/mode) are OPEN. "
  1200	            "Set to a strong random secret ≥ "
  1201	            f"{_MIN_BEARER_LEN} chars before exposing the URL."
  1202	        )
  1203	        (hard_errors if strict else warnings).append(msg)
  1204	
  1205	    # ── Provider key shape ──
  1206	    for field, prefixes in _PROVIDER_PREFIXES.items():
  1207	        value = getattr(settings, field, "") or ""
  1208	        value = value.strip()
  1209	        if not value or not prefixes:
  1210	            continue
  1211	        if not any(value.startswith(p) for p in prefixes):
  1212	            warnings.append(
  1213	                f"{field} doesn't start with any known prefix "
  1214	                f"({'/'.join(prefixes)}); may be a paste error. "
  1215	                f"Loaded as: {_mask(value)}"
  1216	            )
  1217	
  1218	    # ── LLM provider sanity: the *selected* provider must have a
  1219	    #    matching key (unless it's ollama which is local/no-auth, or
  1220	    #    the debug dev profile). ──
  1221	    provider = (settings.llm_provider or "").strip().lower()
  1222	    if provider and provider != "ollama":
  1223	        key_field = f"{provider}_api_key"
  1224	        if not getattr(settings, key_field, ""):
  1225	            msg = (
  1226	                f"llm_provider={provider!r} but {key_field.upper()} "
  1227	                "is empty — every LLM call will fail. Either set the "
  1228	                "key or switch llm_provider to 'ollama'."
  1229	            )
  1230	            (hard_errors if strict else warnings).append(msg)
  1231	
  1232	    # ── T1 sandbox egress consistency ──
  1233	    if settings.t1_allow_egress and not settings.t1_egress_allow_hosts:
  1234	        warnings.append(
  1235	            "t1_allow_egress=true but t1_egress_allow_hosts is empty — "
  1236	            "no hosts will actually be reachable. Did you forget the "
  1237	            "whitelist?"
  1238	        )
  1239	
  1240	    # ── M5: prewarm policy whitelist ──
  1241	    policy = (settings.prewarm_policy or "").strip().lower()
  1242	    if policy not in {"disabled", "shared", "per_tenant"}:
  1243	        msg = (
  1244	            f"OMNISIGHT_PREWARM_POLICY={settings.prewarm_policy!r} is "
  1245	            "invalid. Valid: disabled / shared / per_tenant. "
  1246	            "Defaulting to 'per_tenant' at runtime."
  1247	        )
  1248	        (hard_errors if strict else warnings).append(msg)
  1249	    elif policy == "shared":
  1250	        warnings.append(
  1251	            "OMNISIGHT_PREWARM_POLICY=shared — pre-warmed containers are "
  1252	            "not tenant-bucketed. Only safe on single-tenant or fully-"
  1253	            "trusted deployments. Use 'per_tenant' for SaaS."
  1254	        )
  1255	
  1256	    # ── BP.N.3: web-search provider / budget knobs ──
  1257	    web_search_provider = (settings.web_search_provider or "").strip().lower()
  1258	    if web_search_provider not in {"none", "tavily", "exa", "perplexity"}:
  1259	        msg = (
  1260	            f"OMNISIGHT_WEB_SEARCH_PROVIDER={settings.web_search_provider!r} "
  1261	            "is invalid. Valid: none / tavily / exa / perplexity."
  1262	        )
  1263	        (hard_errors if strict else warnings).append(msg)
  1264	    try:
  1265	        web_search_budget = float(settings.web_search_daily_budget_usd)
  1266	    except (TypeError, ValueError):
  1267	        msg = (
  1268	            "OMNISIGHT_WEB_SEARCH_DAILY_BUDGET_USD must be a non-negative "
  1269	            f"number, got {settings.web_search_daily_budget_usd!r}."
  1270	        )
  1271	        (hard_errors if strict else warnings).append(msg)
  1272	    else:
  1273	        if web_search_budget < 0:
  1274	            msg = (
  1275	                "OMNISIGHT_WEB_SEARCH_DAILY_BUDGET_USD must be non-negative, "
  1276	                f"got {settings.web_search_daily_budget_usd!r}."
  1277	            )
  1278	            (hard_errors if strict else warnings).append(msg)
  1279	
  1280	    # ── Internet-exposure auth (Phase 54 + L1) ──
  1281	    # The single most common foot-gun on first deploy: auth_mode=open
  1282	    # (the default) is fine for a dev box, fatal for an exposed URL.
  1283	    auth_mode = (os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_AUTH_MODE") or "open").strip().lower()
  1284	    if auth_mode not in {"open", "session", "strict"}:
  1285	        hard_errors.append(
  1286	            f"OMNISIGHT_AUTH_MODE={auth_mode!r} unknown. "
  1287	            "Valid: open / session / strict."
  1288	        )
  1289	    elif auth_mode == "open":
  1290	        msg = (
  1291	            "OMNISIGHT_AUTH_MODE=open — the dashboard treats every "
  1292	            "request as admin. Acceptable on a dev box; never on an "
  1293	            "exposed URL. Set OMNISIGHT_AUTH_MODE=strict in prod."
  1294	        )
  1295	        (hard_errors if strict else warnings).append(msg)
  1296	
  1297	    # K1: production environment MUST use strict auth mode.
  1298	    # Exit code 78 (EX_CONFIG from sysexits.h) signals a configuration
  1299	    # error to container orchestrators.
  1300	    env_name = (settings.env or os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_ENV") or "").strip().lower()
  1301	    if env_name == "production" and auth_mode != "strict":
  1302	        hard_errors.append(
  1303	            f"ENV=production requires OMNISIGHT_AUTH_MODE=strict "
  1304	            f"(current: {auth_mode!r}). Refusing to start — exit 78."
  1305	        )
  1306	
  1307	    if env_name == "production" and (settings.docker_runtime or "").strip().lower() != "runsc":
  1308	        hard_errors.append(
  1309	            "ENV=production requires OMNISIGHT_DOCKER_RUNTIME=runsc so "
  1310	            "Tier-1 sandboxes use gVisor. Use non-production env for "
  1311	            "explicit runc compatibility tests."
  1312	        )
  1313	
  1314	    # The bootstrap admin password ships as `omnisight-admin`. Hard-
  1315	    # fail if that's still in use under prod, so an internet-exposed
  1316	    # instance can't have its default admin trivially logged into.
  1317	    # Audit C1 (2026-04-19): both "unset" and "literal default" must be
  1318	    # hard errors under strict mode — the fallback in backend/auth.py
  1319	    # L631 silently lands the well-known credential when the env is
  1320	    # unset, and the must_change_password/428 gate is defence-in-depth,

     1	"""Alembic env — Phase 51.
     2	
     3	OmniSight uses raw aiosqlite (no SQLAlchemy ORM models), so we run
     4	Alembic in offline-friendly mode where each migration carries plain
     5	SQL `op.execute(...)` statements. The `target_metadata = None` line
     6	disables autogenerate; new tables/columns are added by hand-written
     7	migration files.
     8	
     9	The DB URL respects `OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_PATH` env so CI runs
    10	against a per-shard temp file and dev runs against
    11	`data/omnisight.db`.
    12	
    13	OP-865 backwards-compat tag enforcement
    14	---------------------------------------
    15	Every migration generated from ``script.py.mako`` ships a docstring tag
    16	of the form::
    17	
    18	    backwards-compat: <safe|breaking|deprecation-window-1of2|deprecation-window-2of2>
    19	
    20	The placeholder MUST be replaced with one of the four values before the
    21	migration lands. ``scripts/check_migration_compat.py`` parses the tag
    22	from the module docstring and rejects the patchset if missing, unknown,
    23	or still set to the angle-bracket placeholder. See
    24	``docs/operations/migration-deprecation-runbook.md`` for the
    25	two-revision drop-column workflow.
    26	"""
    27	
    28	from __future__ import annotations
    29	
    30	import os
    31	from logging.config import fileConfig
    32	from pathlib import Path
    33	
    34	from alembic import context
    35	from sqlalchemy import create_engine, pool
    36	
    37	config = context.config
    38	
    39	if config.config_file_name is not None:
    40	    fileConfig(config.config_file_name)
    41	
    42	
    43	def _resolve_db_url() -> str:
    44	    # N8: SQLALCHEMY_URL wins so the dual-track validator can point
    45	    # Alembic at a Postgres service container.
    46	    # G4 #2 (HA-04): OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL / DATABASE_URL are next —
    47	    # this is the new connection-abstraction env that the runtime also
    48	    # reads. Alembic itself cannot drive asyncpg (sync engine only), so
    49	    # we coerce `postgresql+asyncpg://` → `postgresql+psycopg2://` here.
    50	    # Falls back to the legacy OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_PATH → sqlite:// path
    51	    # so SQLite-only callers (dev, existing CI jobs) are untouched.
    52	    full = os.environ.get("SQLALCHEMY_URL", "").strip()
    53	    if full:
    54	        return full
    55	    for key in ("OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL", "DATABASE_URL"):
    56	        url = os.environ.get(key, "").strip()
    57	        if url:
    58	            try:
    59	                # Lazy import — keeps env.py usable even if the backend
    60	                # package is not on sys.path (e.g. a minimal alembic tool
    61	                # invocation).
    62	                import sys
    63	                root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
    64	                if str(root) not in sys.path:
    65	                    sys.path.insert(0, str(root))
    66	                from backend.db_url import parse  # type: ignore
    67	            except Exception:  # pragma: no cover — defensive
    68	                return url
    69	            parsed = parse(url)
    70	            return parsed.sqlalchemy_url(sync=True)
    71	    env = os.environ.get("OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_PATH", "").strip()
    72	    if env:
    73	        return f"sqlite:///{env}"
    74	    here = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
    75	    return f"sqlite:///{here / 'data' / 'omnisight.db'}"
    76	
    77	
    78	target_metadata = None
    79	
    80	
    81	def run_migrations_offline() -> None:
    82	    context.configure(
    83	        url=_resolve_db_url(),
    84	        target_metadata=target_metadata,
    85	        literal_binds=True,
    86	        dialect_opts={"paramstyle": "named"},
    87	        render_as_batch=False,
    88	    )
    89	    with context.begin_transaction():
    90	        context.run_migrations()
    91	
    92	
    93	def run_migrations_online() -> None:
    94	    url = _resolve_db_url()
    95	    # Only pre-create a parent directory when the URL is SQLite;
    96	    # Postgres/MySQL URLs point at a network server, not a file path.
    97	    if url.startswith("sqlite:///"):
    98	        Path(url.replace("sqlite:///", "")).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    99	    engine = create_engine(url, poolclass=pool.NullPool)
   100	    with engine.connect() as conn:
   101	        # G4 #1: install the SQLite→Postgres compatibility shim so
   102	        # existing migrations (written against SQLite) run cleanly on
   103	        # Postgres. No-op for SQLite binds.
   104	        # FX.9.2: absolute `backend.` import — keeps `/app` on sys.path
   105	        # rather than `/app/backend`. (Pre-FX.9.3 this also defended
   106	        # against `backend/platform.py` shadowing stdlib `platform`;
   107	        # FX.9.3 renamed the module to `backend/platform_profile.py`
   108	        # so the shadow is gone, but the absolute-import discipline
   109	        # is kept as defence-in-depth — bare `from alembic_pg_compat
   110	        # import …` would still require `/app/backend` on sys.path[0]
   111	        # which we no longer want at all.)
   112	        import sys
   113	        root = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
   114	        if str(root) not in sys.path:
   115	            sys.path.insert(0, str(root))
   116	        from backend.alembic_pg_compat import install_pg_compat  # type: ignore
   117	
   118	        install_pg_compat(conn)
   119	        context.configure(
   120	            connection=conn,
   121	            target_metadata=target_metadata,
   122	            render_as_batch=False,
   123	        )
   124	        with context.begin_transaction():
   125	            context.run_migrations()
   126	
   127	
   128	if context.is_offline_mode():
   129	    run_migrations_offline()
   130	else:
   131	    run_migrations_online()

     1	"""Database persistence layer.
     2	
     3	SQLite path via aiosqlite for the legacy dev flow (no
     4	``OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL`` set). Production runs against PostgreSQL
     5	through :mod:`backend.db_pool` — every domain helper in this module
     6	takes an explicit ``conn`` argument so pool-acquired connections and
     7	SQLite connections share the same call surface.
     8	
     9	Phase-3 Step C.2 (2026-04-21): the PG-path compat shim
    10	``backend.db_pg_compat.PgCompatConnection`` has been retired. The
    11	prod entry point is now the lifespan-managed ``db_pool.init_pool``;
    12	``db.init()`` below only opens the SQLite dev DB. If a
    13	``OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL`` is set, callers are expected to be
    14	pool-native (all domain helpers are), and ``db.init()`` short-circuits.
    15	"""
    16	
    17	from __future__ import annotations
    18	
    19	import json
    20	import logging
    21	import re
    22	from pathlib import Path
    23	from typing import Any
    24	
    25	import aiosqlite
    26	import sqlalchemy as sa
    27	from alembic.ddl.base import AddColumn as _AlembicAddColumn
    28	from sqlalchemy.dialects import sqlite as _sqlite_dialect_mod
    29	
    30	from backend.db_context import (
    31	    tenant_insert_value,
    32	    tenant_where_pg,
    33	)
    34	
    35	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    36	
    37	# FX.1.9: dialect-aware DDL rendering for the in-process SQLite migrator
    38	# below. We render ALTER TABLE statements via SQLAlchemy / alembic's DDL
    39	# compiler instead of f-string interpolation so identifier quoting and
    40	# type/default rendering go through the dialect's escape logic. The
    41	# migration list is hardcoded in source (no user-controlled identifiers),
    42	# but the audit row asked us to take the f-string out of the loop on
    43	# principle — these are the rails the rest of the codebase already uses
    44	# for schema operations (alembic ``op.add_column``).
    45	_SQLITE_DIALECT = _sqlite_dialect_mod.dialect()
    46	_SQLITE_PREPARER = _SQLITE_DIALECT.identifier_preparer
    47	_IDENT_RE = re.compile(r"^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$")
    48	# SQLite-accepted ON DELETE / ON UPDATE actions; reject anything else
    49	# rather than splice an attacker-controlled string into DDL even though
    50	# every current call passes a hardcoded literal.
    51	_FK_ACTIONS = {"CASCADE", "SET NULL", "SET DEFAULT", "RESTRICT", "NO ACTION"}
    52	
    53	
    54	def _validate_ident(name: str) -> str:
    55	    if not _IDENT_RE.match(name):
    56	        raise ValueError(f"Unsafe SQL identifier: {name!r}")
    57	    return name
    58	
    59	
    60	def _render_add_column(table: str, column: sa.Column) -> str:
    61	    """Render ``ALTER TABLE <t> ADD COLUMN <c> ...`` via the SQLite dialect.
    62	
    63	    Uses alembic's ``AddColumn`` DDL construct so identifier quoting,
    64	    type rendering, and default-clause escaping all go through the
    65	    SQLAlchemy compiler instead of f-string composition. SQLite has no
    66	    ``ALTER TABLE ADD CONSTRAINT`` form, so any ``ForeignKey`` on
    67	    ``column`` is appended as an inline ``REFERENCES`` clause built
    68	    through the dialect's ``IdentifierPreparer`` (alembic's AddColumn
    69	    intentionally drops FK because most other dialects need a separate
    70	    ADD CONSTRAINT round-trip — for SQLite the inline form is the only
    71	    option).
    72	    """
    73	    _validate_ident(table)
    74	    _validate_ident(column.name)
    75	    if column.table is None:
    76	        # FK / DDL bookkeeping wants a parent Table; a throwaway one is fine.
    77	        sa.Table(table, sa.MetaData(), column)
    78	    sql = str(_AlembicAddColumn(table, column).compile(dialect=_SQLITE_DIALECT))
    79	
    80	    fk_parts: list[str] = []
    81	    for fk in column.foreign_keys:
    82	        # Use ``target_fullname`` (string form) rather than ``fk.column``
    83	        # so we don't trigger SQLAlchemy's MetaData lookup — the parent
    84	        # tables aren't registered in our throwaway MetaData.
    85	        spec = fk.target_fullname
    86	        if spec.count(".") != 1:
    87	            raise ValueError(f"Unsupported FK target: {spec!r}")
    88	        target_table, target_col = spec.split(".", 1)
    89	        _validate_ident(target_table)
    90	        _validate_ident(target_col)
    91	        clause = (
    92	            f"REFERENCES {_SQLITE_PREPARER.quote(target_table)} "
    93	            f"({_SQLITE_PREPARER.quote(target_col)})"
    94	        )
    95	        if fk.ondelete:
    96	            action = fk.ondelete.upper()
    97	            if action not in _FK_ACTIONS:
    98	                raise ValueError(f"Unsupported ON DELETE action: {fk.ondelete!r}")
    99	            clause += f" ON DELETE {action}"
   100	        if fk.onupdate:
   101	            action = fk.onupdate.upper()
   102	            if action not in _FK_ACTIONS:
   103	                raise ValueError(f"Unsupported ON UPDATE action: {fk.onupdate!r}")
   104	            clause += f" ON UPDATE {action}"
   105	        fk_parts.append(clause)
   106	    if fk_parts:
   107	        sql = f"{sql} {' '.join(fk_parts)}"
   108	    return sql
   109	
   110	def _resolve_db_path() -> Path:
   111	    from backend.config import settings
   112	    if settings.database_path:
   113	        return Path(settings.database_path).expanduser()
   114	    return Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "data" / "omnisight.db"
   115	
   116	
   117	def _resolve_pg_dsn() -> str:
   118	    """Return a libpq-style DSN if OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL / DATABASE_URL
   119	    points at a PG host, else empty string.
   120	
   121	    Accepts the usual ``postgresql+asyncpg://user:pw@host/db`` form and
   122	    strips the ``+asyncpg`` qualifier asyncpg doesn't need. Checked in
   123	    ``OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL`` → ``DATABASE_URL`` precedence matching
   124	    :mod:`backend.db_url.resolve_from_env`.
   125	    """
   126	    import os
   127	    for key in ("OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL", "DATABASE_URL"):
   128	        url = (os.environ.get(key) or "").strip()
   129	        if not url:
   130	            continue
   131	        low = url.lower()
   132	        if low.startswith(("postgresql://", "postgres://", "postgresql+asyncpg://", "postgres+asyncpg://", "asyncpg://")):
   133	            # asyncpg accepts postgresql://... directly; strip driver qualifier.
   134	            for prefix, canon in (
   135	                ("postgresql+asyncpg://", "postgresql://"),
   136	                ("postgres+asyncpg://", "postgresql://"),
   137	                ("asyncpg://", "postgresql://"),
   138	                ("postgres://", "postgresql://"),
   139	            ):
   140	                if low.startswith(prefix):
   141	                    return canon + url[len(prefix):]
   142	            return url  # already postgresql://
   143	    return ""
   144	
   145	
   146	_DB_PATH = _resolve_db_path()
   147	# Holds the aiosqlite connection for the dev-mode path (no PG DSN).
   148	# Production code does NOT read ``_db`` — it goes through
   149	# ``db_pool.get_pool()``. See Phase-3 Step C.2.
   150	_db: Any = None
   151	
   152	
   153	async def init() -> None:
   154	    """Open the SQLite dev DB and create tables if they don't exist.
   155	
   156	    On SQLite (default, no ``OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL``): create tables
   157	    via CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS, run ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN
   158	    migrations in ``_migrate()``, set WAL pragmas.
   159	
   160	    On Postgres (any ``OMNISIGHT_DATABASE_URL`` pointing at PG): this
   161	    function is a no-op. The PG schema is owned by alembic
   162	    (``alembic upgrade head`` runs at deploy time) and the pool is
   163	    opened by the lifespan handler via ``db_pool.init_pool``. The
   164	    Phase-3 Step C.2 cleanup retired the in-process compat wrapper
   165	    that previously coexisted with the pool on the PG path.
   166	    """
   167	    global _db
   168	    pg_dsn = _resolve_pg_dsn()
   169	    if pg_dsn:
   170	        # Production path: schema + pool are owned elsewhere. Nothing
   171	        # to do here — leave ``_db`` as None so anyone who still
   172	        # reaches for it fails fast with the "not initialized" error.
   173	        logger.info(
   174	            "db.init() no-op on PG path — pool lifecycle owned by "
   175	            "db_pool.init_pool, schema owned by alembic."
   176	        )
   177	        return
   178	
   179	    _DB_PATH.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   180	    _db = await aiosqlite.connect(str(_DB_PATH))
   181	    _db.row_factory = aiosqlite.Row
   182	    # SQLite hardening (pragmas must be set before schema creation)
   183	    await _db.execute("PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL")
   184	    await _db.execute("PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000")
   185	    await _db.execute("PRAGMA foreign_keys=ON")
   186	    # Quick integrity check
   187	    async with _db.execute("PRAGMA quick_check") as cur:
   188	        row = await cur.fetchone()
   189	        if row and row[0] != "ok":
   190	            logger.critical("Database integrity check FAILED: %s", row[0])
   191	    await _db.commit()  # Commit pragmas before executescript (which does implicit COMMIT)
   192	    await _db.executescript(_SCHEMA)
   193	    # FTS5 virtual table for L3 episodic memory full-text search
   194	    # (Must be created separately — FTS5 can fail if extension not loaded)
   195	    try:
   196	        await _db.execute("""
   197	            CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE IF NOT EXISTS episodic_memory_fts
   198	            USING fts5(error_signature, solution, soc_vendor, tags, content='episodic_memory', content_rowid='rowid')
   199	        """)
   200	        await _db.commit()
   201	    except Exception as exc:
   202	        logger.warning("FTS5 not available (L3 search will use LIKE fallback): %s", exc)
   203	    # Run lightweight migrations for schema evolution
   204	    await _migrate(_db)
   205	    await _db.commit()
   206	    logger.info("Database ready (WAL mode): %s", _DB_PATH)
   207	
   208	
   209	async def _migrate(conn: aiosqlite.Connection) -> None:
   210	    """Add columns that may be missing in older databases.
   211	
   212	    FX.1.9: column specs are SQLAlchemy ``Column`` objects so the ALTER
   213	    TABLE rendering goes through the SQLite dialect's DDL compiler
   214	    (see :func:`_render_add_column`) rather than f-string composition.
   215	    Numeric defaults use ``sa.text(...)`` to render unquoted (matching
   216	    the prior raw-SQL form ``DEFAULT 0`` / ``DEFAULT 0.0``); string
   217	    defaults are passed as plain Python strings so the compiler quotes
   218	    them (``DEFAULT '...'``).
   219	    """
   220	    _t = sa.Text
   221	    _i = sa.Integer
   222	    _f = sa.Float
   223	    _txt = sa.text
   224	    _fk_proj = lambda: sa.ForeignKey("projects.id", ondelete="SET NULL")  # noqa: E731
   225	
   226	    migrations: list[tuple[str, sa.Column]] = [
   227	        ("agents", sa.Column("sub_type", _t(), nullable=False, server_default="")),

   230	        ("tasks", sa.Column("child_task_ids", _t(), nullable=False, server_default="[]")),
   231	        ("tasks", sa.Column("external_issue_id", _t())),
   232	        ("tasks", sa.Column("issue_url", _t())),
   233	        ("tasks", sa.Column("acceptance_criteria", _t())),
   234	        ("tasks", sa.Column("labels", _t(), nullable=False, server_default="[]")),
   235	        ("tasks", sa.Column("depends_on", _t(), nullable=False, server_default="[]")),
   236	        ("tasks", sa.Column("external_issue_platform", _t())),
   237	        ("tasks", sa.Column("last_external_sync_at", _t())),
   238	        # Pipeline linkage (Phase 46)
   239	        ("tasks", sa.Column("npi_phase_id", _t())),
   240	        # RPG.W20.1 -- multi-task campaign grouping with narrative title.
   241	        ("tasks", sa.Column("rpg_campaign_id", _t())),
   242	        ("tasks", sa.Column("rpg_campaign_title", _t())),
   243	        ("notifications", sa.Column("dispatch_status", _t(), nullable=False, server_default="pending")),
   244	        ("notifications", sa.Column("send_attempts", _i(), nullable=False, server_default=_txt("0"))),
   245	        ("notifications", sa.Column("last_error", _t())),
   246	        # R9 row 2935 (#315) — operational-priority tag (P1/P2/P3).
   247	        # NULLable so legacy callers without severity awareness keep
   248	        # working unchanged; dispatcher consumes this in row 2939.
   249	        ("notifications", sa.Column("severity", _t())),
   250	        # BP.H.3 — persistent red-card marker. DB-backed, not worker
   251	        # global state; every worker reads the same notification row.
   252	        ("notifications", sa.Column(
   253	            "is_red_card", sa.Boolean(), nullable=False,
   254	            server_default=sa.false(),
   255	        )),
   256	        # Artifact version/checksum (Phase 39)
   257	        ("artifacts", sa.Column("version", _t(), nullable=False, server_default="")),
   258	        ("artifacts", sa.Column("checksum", _t(), nullable=False, server_default="")),
   259	        # NPU simulation fields (Phase 36)
   260	        ("simulations", sa.Column("npu_latency_ms", _f(), nullable=False, server_default=_txt("0.0"))),

     6	logs exactly one no-op decision per tick, and stays alive with a heartbeat.

   148	    jira_agent_class: str = "subscription-claude"
   149	    heartbeat_interval_seconds: float = DEFAULT_HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_SECONDS
   150	    tick_interval_seconds: float = DEFAULT_TICK_INTERVAL_SECONDS
   151	    jira_poll_interval_seconds: float = DEFAULT_JIRA_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS
   152	    sweep_interval_seconds: float = DEFAULT_SWEEP_INTERVAL_SECONDS
   153	    event_dedupe_seconds: float = DEFAULT_EVENT_DEDUPE_SECONDS
   154	    acting: bool = False
   155	    cold_start_max_infra: int = DEFAULT_COLD_START_MAX_INFRA
   156	    cold_start_max_reconcile: int = DEFAULT_COLD_START_MAX_RECONCILE
   157	    cold_start_max_sweep: int = DEFAULT_COLD_START_MAX_SWEEP
   158	    cold_start_bot_account_ids: tuple[str, ...] = ()
   159	
   160	    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
   161	        if self.capacity_path is None:
   162	            object.__setattr__(
   163	                self,
   164	                "capacity_path",
   165	                capacity_path_from_env(self.config_dir),
   166	            )
   167	        if self.bridge_events_path is None:
   168	            object.__setattr__(
   169	                self,
   170	                "bridge_events_path",
   171	                self.config_dir / "bridge-events.jsonl",
   172	            )
   173	
   174	    @classmethod
   175	    def from_env(
   176	        cls,
   177	        env: dict[str, str] | None = None,
   178	        *,
   179	        config_dir: Path | None = None,
   180	    ) -> "CoordinatorConfig":
   181	        env = env if env is not None else dict(os.environ)
   182	        base = (
   183	            Path(config_dir)
   184	            if config_dir is not None
   185	            else Path(env.get(CONFIG_DIR_ENV, str(DEFAULT_CONFIG_DIR))).expanduser()
   186	        )
   187	        decision_log_dir = Path(
   188	            env.get(DECISION_LOG_DIR_ENV, str(base / "decision-log"))
   189	        ).expanduser()
   190	        return cls(
   191	            config_dir=base,
   192	            heartbeat_path=base / "heartbeat",
   193	            decision_log_dir=decision_log_dir,
   194	            capacity_path=capacity_path_from_env(base, env),
   195	            bridge_events_path=Path(
   196	                env.get(BRIDGE_EVENTS_FILE_ENV, str(base / "bridge-events.jsonl"))
   197	            ).expanduser(),
   198	            jira_agent_class=env.get(JIRA_AGENT_CLASS_ENV, "subscription-claude"),
   199	            acting=_env_truthy(env.get(ACTING_ENV)),
   200	            cold_start_max_infra=_env_int_from_mapping(
   201	                env, COLD_START_MAX_INFRA_ENV, DEFAULT_COLD_START_MAX_INFRA
   202	            ),
   203	            cold_start_max_reconcile=_env_int_from_mapping(
   204	                env, COLD_START_MAX_RECONCILE_ENV, DEFAULT_COLD_START_MAX_RECONCILE
   205	            ),
   206	            cold_start_max_sweep=_env_int_from_mapping(
   207	                env, COLD_START_MAX_SWEEP_ENV, DEFAULT_COLD_START_MAX_SWEEP
   208	            ),
   209	            cold_start_bot_account_ids=_env_csv_tuple(
   210	                env.get(COLD_START_BOT_ACCOUNT_IDS_ENV)
   211	            ),
   212	        )
   213	
   214	
   215	# ── L1 heartbeat (ADR-0021 §9 L1) ─────────────────────────────────────
   216	
   217	
   218	class HeartbeatWriter:
   219	    """Writes the liveness file every tick (ADR-0021 §9 L1).
   220	
   221	    The watchdog (§9 L2, sibling deploy) polls this file's mtime/content;
   222	    here we only own the *write* side. The write is atomic (temp + rename)
   223	    so a crashed mid-write never leaves a watcher reading a torn file.
   224	    """
   225	
   226	    def __init__(self, path: Path, *, clock: Clock = _utc_now) -> None:
   227	        self._path = Path(path)
   228	        self._clock = clock
   229	
   230	    @property
   231	    def path(self) -> Path:
   232	        return self._path
   233	
   234	    def touch(self) -> datetime:
   235	        """Stamp the heartbeat with the current time + pid. Returns the ts."""
   236	        now = self._clock()
   237	        self._path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True, mode=_DIR_MODE)
   238	        payload = {
   239	            "ts": now.isoformat(),
   240	            "pid": os.getpid(),
   241	            "event": "coordinator-alive",
   242	        }
   243	        tmp = self._path.with_name(self._path.name + f".{uuid.uuid4().hex}.tmp")
   244	        tmp.write_text(json.dumps(payload, sort_keys=True) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
   245	        os.chmod(tmp, _FILE_MODE)
   246	        os.replace(tmp, self._path)
   247	        return now
   248	
   249	
   250	# ── L4 decision log (ADR-0021 §9 L4) ──────────────────────────────────
   251	
   252	
   253	class DecisionLog:
   254	    """Append-only JSONL audit, one file per UTC day (ADR-0021 §9 L4).
   255	
   256	    ``append`` only ever opens the day's file in ``"a"`` mode — it never
   257	    truncates or rewrites — so the log survives crashes and is safe to
   258	    grep / replay for cold-start recovery (§9 L6). Directory is created
   259	    0700 and each day file is chmod'd 0600 on first write.
   260	    """
